XXVI. SECTARIAN REQUESTS
Funds disbursed pursuant to this Agreement may not be expended for any sectarian purpose or
activity, including sectarian worship or instruction in violation of the United States or Arizona
Constitutions.
XXVII. ADVERTISING AND PROMOTION OF AGREEMENT
The Subrecipient shall not advertise or publish information for commercial benefit concerning this
Agreement without the written approval of the AZDOHS.
XXVIII. OWNERSHIP OF INFORMATION, PRINTED AND PUBLISHED MATERIAL
The AZDOHS reserves the right to review and approve any publications funded or partially
funded through this Agreement.  All publications funded or partially funded through this
Agreement shall recognize the AZDOHS and the U.S. Department of Homeland Security.  The
U.S. Department of Homeland Security and the AZDOHS shall have full and complete rights to
reproduce, duplicate, disclose, perform, and otherwise use all materials prepared under this
Agreement.
The Subrecipient agrees that any report, printed matter, or publication (written, visual, or sound, 
but excluding press releases, newsletters, and issue analyses) issued by the Subrecipient 
describing programs or projects funded in whole or in part with Federal funds shall contain the 
following statement: 
"This document was prepared under a grant from the U.S. Department of 
Homeland Security. Points of view or opinions expressed in this document are 
those of the authors and do not necessarily represent the official position or 
policies of the U.S. Department of Homeland Security." 
The Subrecipient also agrees that one copy of any such publication, report, printed matter, or 
publication shall be submitted to the AZDOHS to be placed on file and distributed as appropriate 
to other potential subrecipients or interested parties.  The AZDOHS may waive the requirement 
for submission of any specific publication upon submission of a request providing justification 
from the Subrecipient. 
The AZDOHS and the Subrecipient recognize that research resulting from this Agreement has 
the potential to become public information.  However, prior to the termination of this Agreement, 
the Subrecipient agrees that no research-based data resulting from this Agreement shall be 
published or otherwise distributed in any form without express written permission from the 
AZDOHS and possibly the U.S. Department of Homeland Security.  It is also agreed that any 
report or printed matter completed as a part of this agreement is a work for hire and shall not be 
copyrighted by the Subrecipient. 
XXIX. CLOSED-CAPTIONING OF PUBLIC SERVICE ANNOUNCEMENTS
Any television public service announcement that is produced or funded in whole or in part by the
Subrecipient shall include closed captioning of the verbal content of such announcement.

shall, in all instances, except for Claims arising solely from the negligent or willful acts or 
omissions of the Indemnitee, be indemnified by such contractor from and against any and 
all claims. It is agreed that such contractor will be responsible for primary loss 
investigation, defense and judgment costs where this indemnification is applicable.  
Additionally on all applicable insurance policies, contractor and its subcontractors shall 
name the State of Arizona, and its departments, agencies, boards, commissions, 
universities, officers, officials, agents, and employees as an additional insured and also 
include a waiver of subrogation in favor of the State. 
XXXI. TERMINATION
a) All parties reserve the right to terminate the Agreement in whole or in part due to the failure of
the Subrecipient or AZDOHS to comply with any term or condition of the Agreement, to
acquire and maintain all required insurance policies, bonds, licenses, and permits or to make
satisfactory progress in performing the Agreement.  The staff of either party shall provide a
written thirty (30) day advance notice of the termination and the reasons for it.
b) If the Subrecipient chooses to terminate the Agreement before the grant deliverables have
been met then the AZDOHS reserves the right to collect all reimbursements distributed to the
Subrecipient.
c) The AZDOHS may, upon termination of this Agreement, procure, on terms and in the manner
that it deems appropriate, materials or services to replace those under this Agreement.  The
Subrecipient shall be liable to the AZDOHS for any excess costs incurred by the AZDOHS in
procuring materials or services in substitution for those due from the Subrecipient.
XXXII. CONTINUATION OF PERFORMANCE THROUGH TERMINATION
The Subrecipient shall continue to perform, in accordance with the requirements of the
Agreement, up to the date of termination, as directed in the termination notice.
XXXIII. PARAGRAPH HEADINGS
The paragraph headings in this Agreement are for convenience of reference only and do not
define, limit, enlarge, or otherwise affect the scope, construction, or interpretation of this
Agreement or any of its provisions.

XXXIV. COUNTERPARTS
This Agreement may be executed in any number of counterparts, copies, or duplicate originals.
Each such counterpart, copy, or duplicate original shall be deemed an original, and collectively
they shall constitute one agreement.
XXXV. AUTHORITY TO EXECUTE THIS AGREEMENT
Each individual executing this Agreement on behalf of the Subrecipient represents and warrants
that he or she is duly authorized to execute this Agreement.
XXXVI. SPECIAL CONDITIONS
a) The Subrecipient must comply with the most recent version of the Administrative
Requirements, Cost Principles, and Audit requirements.
b) The Subrecipient acknowledges that the DHS and the AZDOHS reserve a royalty-free, non-
exclusive, and irrevocable license to reproduce, publish, or otherwise use, and authorize

under an award or sub-award; and (2) any rights of copyright to which a subrecipient
purchases ownership with Federal support. The Subrecipient shall consult with the AZDOHS
regarding the allocation of any patent rights that arise from, or are purchased with, this
funding.
c) The Subrecipient agrees to cooperate with any assessments, state/national evaluation efforts,
or information or data collection requests, including, but not limited to, the provision of any
information required for the assessment or evaluation of any activities within this agreement.
d) The Subrecipient is prohibited from transferring funds between programs (e.g., State
Homeland Security Program, Urban Area Security Initiative, Operation Stonegarden).

Board of Supervisors Regular 4.a.        
Meeting Date: 10/09/2018  
Title: Hopi DOT Request for Road Maintenance Assistance
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: John Osgood, Public Works Director
Department: Public Works
Presented By: John Osgood
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of Task Agreement number seven to Memorandum of Agreement with Hopi Tribe
Department of Transportation (HDOT) for road improvement assistance on a segment of
inventoried roadway identified as BIA Route 508 serving the First Mesa Youth Center and
Navajo County Public Library in Polacca, Arizona. 
Background:
The First Mesa Youth Center in Polacca, Arizona opened its doors in 2015.  In addition to youth
services which include an after school program, educational and recreational activities, and a
computer lab provided by Center staff, the Navajo County Public Library system will be
providing library services at the Center, serving not only Polacca, but reservation-wide areas
including neighboring Navajo communities.  Over the past few months, staff from the Navajo
County Library District, Public Works, the First Mesa Youth Center and the Hopi Department of
Transportation have been discussing ways to improve access to the Center.  Specifically, there is
an approximately 500' segment of BIA Route 508 leading into the Center that is very difficult to
drive on and is in need of repair.
As indicated in Task Agreement number 7 and in the attached letters of request,  the Hopi
Department of Transportation is requesting maintenance assistance with the repair project.  The
work involves Navajo County staff purchasing,  transporting and placing approximately 300 tons
of milling material and a binder product used in the placement the millings to extend the life of
the road surface.  HDOT staff will assist with traffic control and other project related activities.  
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends approval of Task Agreement number 7 to the MOA with the Hopi Tribe
Department of Transportation
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 2019
Budgeted Y/N: Yes
Amount Requested: $28,000

<!-- page 445 -->
Fiscal Impact:
Funds are budgeted in the 5 Year Capital Improvement Projects for the Hopi Tribe.

Task Agreement No. 7 
to Memorandum of Agreement 
Hopi Tribe Department of Transportation 
 
 
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors, by motion duly passed at the Board’s regular 
meeting on  October 9, 2018  , has approved the expenditure of up to $ 28,000.00 in (  ) 
Special Project Road Funds, (  )  TEA 21 Funds, or (X) Highway User Revenue Funds 
(check appropriate box) (“Allocated Funds”) to be used for the purchase of _MC250 
Binder  and funding the Navajo County Road Crew who will be transporting _ 300_ tons 
of milling material and performing road maintenance on Hopi Tribe’s public roads which 
is located on the Hopi Indian Reservation, in Navajo County, within Supervisorial District 
No. 2 of the County. Such maintenance duties may include, but are not limited  to: 
operating motor grader, water truck, end loader, trucks or backhoe to accomplish 
removing or placing milling material; applying binder or water to road material. The 
Allocated Funds shall be used to supplement other monies that are or may become 
available to the Tribe for road maintenance purposes on Hopi Tribe’s public roads in that 
portion of the Hopi Indian Reservation within Navajo County, Supervisorial District No. 2 
of the County. The Tribe understands and acknowledges that the Allocated Funds ar e 
monies that the County has received from the Arizona Highway User Revenue Fund 
(HURF) and which are subject to audit by the Arizona Auditor General. 
 
The Tribe warrants and represents that the __MC250 Binder_ purchased and the Navajo 
County Road Crew’s maintenance duties will be funded with the Allocated Funds where 
it will be used primarily for maintenance activities on roads within District 2, which are 
open to the public and are eligible for the expenditure of HURF monies under the Arizona 
Constitution and related Arizona Revised Statutes.  The Hopi DOT agrees to fully 
cooperate in any audit of the Allocated Funds by the Arizona Auditor General. 
 
The receipt of the Allocated Funds shall not cause other monies to be diverted from 
projects within this area or allocated to projects outside this area. 
 
The County shall have no responsibility or liability whatsoever after reapplication of 
millings is complete. The Tribe’s Department of Transportation shall be responsible for 
identifying, prioritizing, scheduling, and supervising all maintenance activities for which 
the Allocated Funds are used in whole or in part.  It is understood that the County’s sole 
obligation hereunder is to provide a one-time maintenance service with the Allocated 
Funds associated with Task Agreement No. 7. 
 
Any Allocated Funds that are unused as of June 30, 2019 , shall no longer be available 
for distribution pursuant to this Task Agreement. 
 
The Allocated Funds under this agreement shall be paid by Navajo County directly to the 
invoicing party upon the County’s receipt of invoices or billing statement that show that 
the items have been purchased and approved by the Tribe. The total of all invoices shall 
not exceed the approved amount from this agreement.

Navajo County 
Navajo County Supervisor District II, Mr. Jesse Thompson,

Re: Request for Navajo County assistance for improvement on inventoried road. 
 
 
Honorable Jesse Thompson, 
 
It is with pleasure that I write this supporting request letter on behalf of our Youth Center 
for attachment along with Mr. Michael Lomayaktewa’s letter for road improvemen t 
assistance, identified as BIA Route 508. 
 
Since 2015, we had the opportunity to open our doors as a Youth Center here in First Mesa, 
Polacca, Arizona, located northwest of Hwy 264, MP 392.  
We have been fortunate to be funded under the umbrella of HUD, the Hopi Housing 
Authority and have been successful in surviving this long. We have a computer lab area, we 
provide a monthly schedule of comprehensive educational and recreational activities, an 
after-school program, along with community education and information services. We are 
now also bringing the County Library service into our center to provide a Hopi Library 
services not only to Polacca, but reservation-wide from Moencopi to Yuwehloo, including 
Navajo neighboring communities. 
 
Since coming on board, my objective is to build and improve this center so the road was 
one of the first projects to address. So discussions have been initiated since 2016 in 
learning the history of the place, determining whether this was still on the Hopi inventory 
list and working with HDOT, Mr. Michael Lomayaktewa, establishing support and approval 
from Village and clan Leaders, including working with Realty Office to acquire all the 
necessary documents, Legal studies, to ensure we had all the proper documentations. 
Movement was going but was slow until when we brought the Public library in, that is 
when Ms. Geneva Durkee, from the County Library office started buffering the Navajo 
County office and so with the meeting on 9/26/2018 here at the FMYC with all parties, it 
was a successful and productive meeting. 
 So from that time on to this point, discussions have taken place between parties and now 
we formally request to your office for assistance to complete this road improvement 
project, BIA Route 508 to the First Mesa Youth Center here in Polacca, Arizona. With 
partnerships in place, HURF funds and responsibilities identified, we now move forward

October 1, 2018 
 
 
Supervisor Thompson and Members of the Board of Supervisors: 
 
 
The Hopi Public Library, upon the invitation of the First Mesa Youth Center in Polacca, will soon be 
opening a branch library in a room in the Youth Center, which will be named the Hopi Public Library 
Polacca Branch. A signed MOU between the Hopi Department of Education and the Youth Center is in 
place. Shelving and library collections are already set up. 
 
Navajo County Library District and the Arizona State Library recognize that this branch library will be a 
viable library location open to the public. 
 
The short road from Highway 264 leading to the entrance of the Youth Center/Branch Library is in 
terrible shape and is very rough for any vehicle driving up that hill, which is a concern for the increased 
traffic by library users. I am also concerned that the uneven surface could pose a hazard for any 
pedestrian or bicyclist coming to use the library or may be a deterrent.  
 
The improvement to the road with the assistance of Navajo County Public Works would be a very 
worthwhile project to benefit the branch library, the youth center and Polacca Village. 
 
 
Sincerely, 
 
 
Geneva Durkee 
County Librarian

Board of Supervisors Regular 4.b.1.a.        
Meeting Date: 10/09/2018  
Title: Flood Control District - Community Rating System Annual Recertification
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Bill Bess, County Engineer/Assistant Public Works Director
Department: Public Works
Motion before the Board:
Community Rating System Recertification letter
Background:
Navajo County participates in the National Flood Insurance Program (NFIP).Compliance with this
program allows Navajo County citizens to purchase Flood Insurance underwritten by the NFIP.
Navajo County has previously elected to participate in the NFIP program known as the
Community Rating System. The Community Rating System allows communities to exceed the
minimum NFIP requirements and as a result to enjoy discounts on the Flood Insurance Premiums.
The Community Rating System requirements are focused on adopting regulatory requirements for
land use and development that exceed the NFIP minimum. Currently Navajo County's rating has
resulted in a 10% discount in flood insurance premiums. The Flood Control team has prepared the
necessary information and is ready to submit for the annual recertification. Our submittal should
qualify Navajo County for a continued 10% reduction in Flood Insurance premiums for Navajo
County citizens.
